My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XXI/M/M_00195/index.shtml -- 195/M]] How many percent of the savings of 27 billion schillings per year from the administrative reform you celebrated last year will actually come about in the current year?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3413	I'm asking for a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3429	Mr President. Deputies! High house! The number of 27 billion schilling has not been known to me, but it has always been called 21.21 billion schilling. I am very happy to tell you how this figure is composed: from the savings of the federal-state agreements on administrative reform of € 260 million or 3.61 billion schilling, which is the area we have negotiated with the national leaders in the so-called rear round, from savings in the ministerial administration of € 190 million or 2.6 billion schilling as well as from personal policies to a level of € 1.1 billion or 15 billion schilling. I would like to tell you that yesterday we had the interim report on the reduction of personnel in the Council of Ministers. The target of 15 000 full employment equivalents to be reduced has already reached two thirds, so that it is expected that the full volume will be implemented by the end of the legislative period.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3456	Additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3471	Since these savings do not happen through savings – yesterday, an increase in penalties was decided – because here more revenue of 198 million shilling appears on your paper, so that additional resources are lurked by the citizens on the revenue side, but not savings potentials, I may ask you: [[Abg. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3498	Please, Mrs. Vice chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3513	Of the sum of 21.21 billion schilling I mention, there is no single amount based on revenue, but they are purely expenditure-side measures. That means we're saving in the administrative sector. We have set ourselves very ambitious targets, also through savings in the individual department administrations. We have administrative procedures which are becoming shorter, thus creating a cost relief for the public. You know that in connection with the euro conversion, the federal government has given the guarantee that it will not be rounded up. This has also been observed – unfortunately not by all countries to the extent that we have done so. The savings resulting from the administrative reform are exclusively and only expenditure-side measures, that is, savings made by the administration itself, there is no burden on a citizen.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3626	Mrs. Vice-Chancellor! How far have the deregulation measures been advanced by the countries?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3642	Please, Mrs. Vice chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3657	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) In the last few weeks, we have taken up the state of play from all countries and have received very extensive explanations of the deregulation measures in the countries. In all federal states, the foundations for implementing the 2001 Administrative Reform Act have been created in the best way and partly already. In six federal states, state administration reform laws have already been adopted or are being prepared to implement the priorities of the Federal Administrative Reform Act in national fields. This includes, in particular, the one-stop shop principle, i.e. the summary of a public contact point and the transfer of skills to the district authorities, the UVS as a second instance and thus a significant reduction in administrative procedures. In all federal states, efforts are being made to transfer the building authorities' competences. This has already been done in some federal states, such as Salzburg. There are also numerous initiatives to deregulation and improve eGovernment. I have made available from all the federal states the detailed arrangements that have already been made. It would now lead too far to bring the entire report from all nine federal states, but I can send it to you in writing.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3867	I'm asking for a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3883	Deputies! We are particularly concerned about the motivation of employees in relation to administrative reform measures, since a reform without employees would not be possible at all. The administrative reform measures I mentioned above are only possible through the contribution and commitment of many employees of the public service. Regrettably, however, it always occurs that individual political groups, for example by the trade union, are trying to insure the employees. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3915	Additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3930	Mrs. Vice chancellor! In this context, the final position is also discussed again and again. What considerations do you have in order to ensure this important protection, particularly in politically sensitive areas, when reorganising the law of the service?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3946	I'm asking for a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e3961	The common aim of the Federal Government is to create a modern, flexible and performance-oriented service right for the public service in Austria, i.e. to introduce a performance-oriented salary for employees in particular. We currently have a salary system that is immensely complicated and depressive in many areas and does not promote performance. Pragmatization is discussed in a very emotional way. I believe that it is important that we protect the employees of the public service in their role from being exposed to political access. This will be absolutely secure in the future. This means that no employee will be exposed to political influence in the future. This is also a matter of concern in the law. As a model, we have the Federal Employment Law of Switzerland, where, in cooperation with the trade unions and staff representatives, a service right has been developed, which provides all necessary protection measures for employees. It will also ensure that we have a flexible labour market, that we do not have different classes of workers in Austria, but a labour market with the same rules for everyone. We also want to facilitate the change between public service and private sector in the future.

My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XXI/M/M_00193/index.shtml -- 193/M]] What measures do you consider necessary to improve the service situation of contract teachers who are threatened with non-renewal employment annually or with the reduction of their teaching obligation?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4361	I'm asking for a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4376	Deputies! You know that there is a pay schedule of your own, namely the II-L scheme, for these contract teachers. This applies, for example, to the use to represent a person specifically appointed, to use in objects created at a school within the framework of their school autonomy, or to use in open-ended objects and in non-binding exercises. The special feature of this scheme is that the arrangement of temporary contracts is allowed to go beyond that provided for in other fields of service for more than seven years. This is often used in contracts relating to a school year or a representation case, whereby the hourly scale may naturally be subject to deviations up or down in the event of a new use. Due to the staff structures and the demand fluctuations that can only be very influenced by the service provider's side of the staff – because these are parameters that we cannot control – the teaching administration has always stressed that increased flexibility in terms of time-limitation compared to the general law is necessary, because it is not possible to calculate the demand precisely for years. We repeatedly criticised schooling institutions make such special arrangements necessary in a different direction. If we had more flexibility, for example, in the area of school-based institutions, this problem would be much easier to solve, but we have a system that is very difficult to implement in practice. However, I believe that an appropriate balance of interests has been achieved between the understandable desire for a stable employment relationship and the particular organisational conditions of a school business and efficient management. There is also a very special importance here for the assessment and the interest of teachers and countries.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4729	The 4th. Mr Krüger. — (DE) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to thank Mr Krüger for his answer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4747	Dear Mrs. Vice chancellor! My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XXI/M/M_00190/index.shtml -- 190/M]] What progress has been made in the field of e-government last year?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4775	I'm asking for a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4801	It is gratifying that Austria is at the forefront of all countries in the European comparison, as far as e-government is concerned. We set up a so-called ICT board at government level a year ago in order to pool the strategic objectives in each department. We have adopted a very fragmented system in which each department had its own ICT orientations. We have now summarized these areas. We thus unite the use of information and communication technology and also improve coordination. We have a very successful project work with the local authorities, i.e. with countries, cities and communities in this context, but also with stakeholders such as the Chamber of Commerce and the Industrial Association, with state companies and institutions of the federal government such as the Bundesrechenzentrum or the Bundesanstalt Statistik Austria, but also with the Court of Auditors and the Parliamental Directorate. The aim is to set common objectives, for example with a view to a uniform form. This is a major relief for the citizen. We still have a very fragmented form in this area. Access through electronic signature is a question of the future, and Austria is also a leader in this field across Europe. The introduction of the electronic file, which is now in place in the individual departments, will allow for the simultaneous processing of an act in several places, thus significantly shortening procedures for the public and bringing clear relief and cost savings to the administration as well.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4829	Would you like an additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4844	Is there any other practical application of e-government from the point of view of the citizen?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4859	Please, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4875	Our objective is to be able to challenge all official routes electronically by the year 2005. This is a very important area. Already today a number of applications are possible. For example, we have implemented targeted measures with help-business for small and medium-sized enterprises in the field of business registration, as well as other applications. We have installed an internationally award-winning project, namely the official helper online help.gv.at, about which the citizen, so to speak, for all the areas of life, for all the contact points he has with the administration, also gets the corresponding information. The notification information has already been implemented in this context, local tax declaration, loss report, event application, commercial application for individual companies and companies as well. We have this year the user-friendly and, above all, disabled access to all these measures in implementation. We have a cooperation with the countries. Especially in the field of justice – and you know this, honourable Member – there are particularly excellent projects in the international field. The basic book is understandable, which will bring considerable relief to the parties, but of course also to lawyers and notaries, and thus will also lead to cost savings, especially for the individual citizens. In the first quarter of next year, we will have implemented the electronic settlement of the wage and income tax declaration in the area of finance online. This is also a major relief and reduction of procedures.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4909	Additional question? – Mrs Mag. Hakl, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4924	Dear Mrs. Vice-Chancellor! It will be much more possible for the citizen to take office from home. How is the security of applications in the network and on these electronic data routes guaranteed?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4943	I'm asking for a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4958	I believe that security and data protection in this area are a very central issue. We have created legal bases in cooperation with experts and also with the European Union in order to ensure that there can be no abuse in Austria. Especially the electronic signature is an important point in this context, with which we have set up appropriate measures with many experts. But it is still a second area which I would like to mention in this context: It is particularly important to us that we also allow access to those groups of people who do not have a PC at home or who do not belong to the information technology society. We have therefore entered into a very successful cooperation with the Austrian Trafiken within the framework of the Trafiknet, as we call it, within the framework of which those people who do not have this access at home can benefit in relatively simple and direct ways.

The federal government is therefore forced to compensate for the already existing considerable utilization deficits in order to minimise the economic damage. I would like to say once again that the procedure that was then chosen with the lease agreement, which at least includes an annual rent for Laudon Castle of around €1.4 million, is a process that, in my opinion, was always irresponsible to the economic and taxpayers, in other words, about 19 million schilling. We will do everything we can to minimise these costs as far as possible, but contracts concluded by the Republic at the time are valid and cannot simply be resolved.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e6965	Dear Mrs. Vice-Chancellor! What further reform measures have you taken to relieve the executive and give officials more time to work on the streets?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e6980	Please, Mrs. Vice chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e6996	Our aim is, as you have correctly said, to relieve executive officials, who receive special training and are specifically qualified, from purely administrative activities. This is what we have done in many areas, for example, the passport, reporting and discovery system, where the transfer of these agendas to the respective magistrates has taken place and thus also the capacity for the executive service has become free. We have broken down double-tracks between security directors and district authorities under the association law, which have also caused a huge administrative burden. We have implemented an automation-supported central association register. We have a better computerization of the gendarmeries and cooperation with the district authorities, which has not yet been established. We have implemented the reduction of administrative units through the reform of the Gendarmerie Posts and the National Army Commanders in the field of the Ministry of the Interior, where it is our aim to really employ executives where it is necessary to relieve the people's safety, and as far as possible from all administrative agents.

It has the status of European law and is open to appeal before the European Court of Justice. We have enshrined in this Brussels Treaty the 21 security failures and their repairs and also the seven major complexes and their repairs. The Austrian Federal Government's strategy is very clear: On the basis of our common attitude to have an AFC-free Austria, we aim first at closing the high-risk reactors in Europe. In this case, the cooling pipes would have to take over the steam cooling. Unfortunately, this is not possible in Temelín, because the cooling pipes have been moved directly next to the steam conduction. In the event of an explosion, it will therefore come to a GAU. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e13111	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! The new Federal Air Re Content Act constitutes a desirable resolution of the previously unclear legal situation in this area and a further step towards administrative simplification, which means: Merging the country-specific regulations to an extent that is useful and necessary for air conservation, ensuring the high environmental standards in Austria – standards that are in the top international field –, resolving legal uncertainties and a lesser level of administration. It is a shame, ladies and gentlemen, that particularly representatives of the Greens and also Mrs Sima of SPÖ are once again trying to use these sensible solutions to put the Austrian peasantry to the prangle and to present the small number of EIA procedures in the agricultural farms as "environmental sins". Let me make this clear: Austrian agriculture – like all other farms – is subject to the EIA. And this must also be carried out; it must not be carried out only if it is smaller holdings. I would have liked to have seen in practice the number of animals with the 60 000 pigs that Members-Collegine Petrovic had previously wanted to explain to me, but you have spoken of 60 000 pieces of cattle. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Petrovic.]] The thresholds for implementing this very comprehensive review are 20 to 30 percent lower in agriculture in Austria than the EU Directive provides. Furthermore, the cumulation provision laid down in the law leads Austria's farmers to have to carry out an environmental impact assessment with far smaller livestock stocks, if other animal owners are also in the vicinity. – In other words, a much sharper interpretation of this directive for Austrian agriculture! Ladies and gentlemen! In Austria, however, despite 20 and 30 percent of lower values, stricter directives, and additional cumulation provisions, there are only individual cases of EIA testing – and this only results from the fact that Austria's agriculture is much more small-structured than it is in Europe. The following figures: In Austria, averaging holdings have on average 40 pieces of cattle, in the Netherlands, also known as an EU country, on average 700 pieces of cattle. – You try to draw such a scenario from the opposition parties also for Austria and thus to give Austrian agriculture to the pranger – and we really cannot accept that! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Dear ladies and gentlemen! Let me also focus on the delicate issue of economic fertilizers: Economic fertilisers represent a basic prerequisite for a circular economy, actually the most important capital of a farmer – whether it be a organic farmer or a normal farm. The fact that a farmer is highly interested in dealing with his fertiliser efficiently, with this important raw material, is a matter of course! It would therefore be grossly inappropriate to interpret air content provisions so restrictive that this would mean that economic fertilisers could no longer be used and replaced by chemical fertilizers. Mrs Glawischnig, I have already explained to you that there is no differentiation between agricultural and industrial holdings in this respect, because that is a question of scale! And since it is with us – thank God! – many smaller farms do not pose a problem! It's as simple as that. You can also read this in the documents. [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig: Smaller businesses have to do this...!]] I am convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that this is a very sensible solution, because on the one hand, administrative simplification is needed – and on the other hand, house understanding is also required in legislation. – Thank you for your attention.

This year, very important points have already been decided here in the House, such as the amendment of the Waste Management Act, which was adopted by two-thirds of the majority and which brings a new perspective to waste management. In this context, I am also thinking of the Environmental Management Act, which brings about improvements for businesses and the environment. I should also like to mention the amendment of the Environmental Promotion Act, which also takes due account of the content of the Climate Change Convention in respect of the Kyoto ratification process. However, I would also like to mention the Forest Act, which has brought a clearer orientation towards sustainability. This path is recognised by the Austrian population. After all, 78 percent of the population say that they are "very" or "very satisfied" with the environmental situation in Austria. This is also internationally recognized, which we can see when we observe, for example, the various rankings that Austria has in a leading position in the international scene. Ladies and gentlemen! Now I would like to comment briefly on the two legislative proposals and also to say a few words on the debate on the amendment. First of all, the Federal Air Content Act: I am convinced that this is a very essential milestone, which we are putting here with this legalisation, and we are taking an essential step towards administrative simplification with the national harmonisation in this area and thus all of us have the same level of information. Ladies and gentlemen! This law alone eliminates eleven national regulations and seven regulations. Again: Therefore, an important step towards administrative simplification. On the subject of agriculture, I would like to take up what you, Mrs Glawischnig, have said here, because, in my view, this represents a very clear contradiction in which you are acting argumentatively here. Mrs Glawischnig, you are saying that there is a derogation for industrial-commercial installations under the German Air Content Act with an air-resource permit. That's welcome. Furthermore, you are of the opinion that in the case of agricultural holdings which have to have an environmental impact assessment, the EIA also takes into account the air-responsible issues. I agree with you that far. – But do you know what your mistake is? They say that all agricultural holdings that are below this limit of the EIA would be subject to the full regime of air law, and that they would affect all farms: the small farms, the medium-sized farms, which would not have any equal treatment with those farms that have an EIA duty, or the industrial-commercial farms which have an air-result authorisation, Mrs Glavischnig. [[Interlocutory calls from Dr. Glawischnig and Heinzl.]] Therefore, this Committee has now been decided on in the Committee on the Environment. You have asked, Mr Heinzl, what is "ordinary agricultural management"? . The Committee itself has defined that in the Committee. As a minister responsible for the environment and agriculture, I am telling you that from both areas of responsibility I can say absolutely yes to this amendment, because it takes into account, on the one hand, the environmental aspect and its objective, but on the other, that it does not lead to any deterioration of the agricultural holdings that properly operate. I therefore believe that with your argument, Mrs Glavischnig – I would like to draw your attention to this – you should fall into a trap, because your amendment would have a negative impact on these small and medium-sized farmers, which is why I do not understand. [[New calls from MEPs Dr. Glawischnig and Heinzl.]] As far as the radiation protection law is concerned, I would like to thank you for your broad support. It is true that we also discussed in the Committee that we do not have a final decision on the question of final storage, as is the case in no other state in the world. I believe that Austria is acting correctly when it comes to this issue in cooperation with other European countries, which, in our view, have the level of environmental responsibility and safety responsibility that allows us to take responsibility for a common solution. In this sense, this Austrian path of European cooperation is correct, economically wise and well justified against the background of security requirements. Thank you.

[[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We should therefore benefit from the parliamentary discussion to recognise in its broadest sense the conservation of the natural composition of the air, which we guarantee better and more clearly by means of this new law. The health and well-being of people, but also the life of plants and animals, depend on whether we protect our air sufficiently from contamination. In addition to the numerous measures that the Federal Government has set out in the Kyoto Protocol for implementation and which we fully support in terms of content, the uniform federal regulation of the obligation to maintain air pollution and to prohibit the burning of non-biogenic materials outside heating systems is also an essential part of our modern climate protection policy. Our particular thanks go to our Environment Minister, yes – I would like to say – Minister of Life, Willi Molterer. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] In my opinion, it is not only legal and administrative adjustments – especially the B-VG-Novelle 1998 – but it is also a clear sign that the state is generally committed to the clean-up of our air and therefore assumes responsibility. In addition to the Federal Air Re Content Act, we also adopt the Radiation Protection EU Adjustment Act. There too, it could be inclined to take this as a purely technical adaptation to EU practices. – Far from it! The new law brings important changes to dose limits for professionally exposed people as well as for the entire population. This means that we normize the legal protection of people against excessive radiation pollution. However, the explicit ban on the addition of radioactive substances to foods, toys and cosmetics also contributes to the improved protection of public health. For a layman, these changes of law may be of course, since no one wants to be maliciously polluting our air or endangering people by excessive radioactive radiation; as political representatives of our people, however, we must ensure that people in our country are protected from such dangerous activities. It is our task to protect the lives and health of the Austrians and to shape environmental policy within the meaning of this objective. With our Environment Minister Willi Molterer, this goal and responsibility are in good hands!

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e14397	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The protocols to be adopted today of the Alpine Convention now require our country and also the EU to become a comprehensive, cross-sectoral policy in areas such as transport, tourism, air conservation, mountain forests and soil protection, as well as water management and waste management. The Alpine Convention gives the Alpine region an ecologically special status within the European Union. For me as a Tyrolean and for all Austria, this is in the VI. The Alpine Conference of Lucerne reached a very important core area of this Convention in the transport protocols. For our country, the key principle of the Convention is the abandonment of the construction of new high-level roads for cross-country traffic. This is of particular importance and therefore must mean that the Alpine countries do not abandon new transit routes, that the Alemagna motorway from Upper Italy towards East Tyrol is not realized and that no new transit route through the remote and via the long-distance pass may be built, since the A 7 is already built up to the Austrian border. In particular, the Transport Protocol provides the Minister of the Environment and the Minister of Transport with a very important back cover for the implementation of urgent Austrian concerns, such as the transit agreement and the ecopoints regime. The Federal Government is therefore urgently invited to remain strong in the transit agreement and to negotiate an adequate successor agreement with the EU within the meaning of the Alpine Convention. There are braking methods and pre-emptive obedience to the EU, as Mr Bundeskanzler Schüssel, unfortunately, repeatedly shows him, as unnecessary as a pneumonia, ladies and gentlemen, because his requirements for the EU, for the truck's upper limit, for the 108 per cent clause, are not in the sense of our country. In doing so, the Federal Chancellor and the Federal Government of the EU give the impression that they themselves do not believe in an extension of the transit agreement. In doing so, he and the Federal Government give a judgment also adopted by the ECJ, in which it recognises the transit contract as an exemplary contract for the protection of the population. [[Federal Minister Mag. Molterer: Stay with the truth!]] Federal Minister! We live in a country with a total of 26 000 kilometres of transit routes that travel through the natural landscape of the Alps. In this Alpine region, an average of 100 million tourists are looking for recreation annually – in the valleys, but also in the mountains. In Austria alone there are 18 000 skiers and 2 400 cable cars in the Alps. In the sense of the Alpine Convention, it is important to find the balance between the ecological, economic and social needs in the Alpine region. The Alpine Convention is a binding treaty for the entire Alpine region, which aims at sustainable economics and a balance between economy and ecology. Austria has shown a great deal of commitment in the past ten years until today's decision-making of the Alpine Convention. However, this commitment must continue. It is, however, unfortunate to note that the EU is no longer as committed to the Alpine Convention as it used to be. From this point of view, however, I would like to express our thanks to all those who have worked in this Treaty. It is in particular all environmental associations and the Alpenverein and, in particular, Mr Mag. Peter Hasslacher. Without their pressure and commitment, this form of the Alpine Convention would not have been achieved. Finally, I would like to ask Mr Mag. Cite hatred as a reminder to the Federal Government. In a preface to the Alpine Convention, he noted: "With the exception of the tough fight for the final fixation of new high-level roads for alpine traffic in the context of the Alpine Convention and the heroic struggle of the Transit Forum Austria-Tyrol, there is no, as always, outstanding emphasis on an environmentally friendly transport policy at the state level in Austria." Ladies and gentlemen! That's all that says: "There are no, as always, outstanding accents on an environmentally friendly transport policy in Austria," Mag. Hate laugher said it. Ladies and gentlemen! The Alpine Convention is not only a back cover for our government and an important basis for all further negotiations with the EU, but also a sample of nails and a credibility test for the implementation of Austrian Alpine policy.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e14459	Mr President. Federal Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The Alpine region is a very sensitive and sustainable habitat. The task of politics is to give this habitat the future. I am very pleased to be able to decide today eight out of twelve protocols dedicated to this Alpine region. In my civil profession, I deal with the sustainable design of habitats, in particular with sustainable development. I would like to address a few areas of this Alpine Convention today. The first area is tourism. When I read that it is ultimately about creating a balance of interests between the environment and the economy, I would like to say that this is a very big challenge, because it is ultimately also about respecting the needs of the resident population and visitors. Here I can read in the summary that it will be necessary to carry out the designation of resting zones not only for the animal and plant world, but ultimately also for the native population, because there is also the loss of culture, tradition and customs where borders and sustainabilitys of this habitat are exceeded. That is why we can attach a high value to this protocol, because it represents a first step in the right direction of sustainability in the consideration of these interests. A second essential area is the area of spatial planning. We can see that the Alpine region is a rural area and that, unfortunately, in Austria, we have to fight God with the problem of dispersal. We also have to fight for, among other things, climate change, the protection of forests to the extent that we need them no longer exist. There is a great need for policy, especially in the field of agriculture and forestry, for us to ensure that our cultural landscape and in particular our mountain forest and protected forest are preserved and that sustainable agriculture and forestry is maintained. Another area of this Alpine Convention concerns the field of energy. I can tell you that in Austria we are in a happy position to find a high level of renewable energy sources within the Alpine region. Together, ladies and gentlemen, we have opted for the better variant, knowing that the variants in the price are closely interconnected, knowing that the Eurofighter is the scarcely more expensive solution, but also knowing that it offers the advantage of bringing a European solution to Austria, that, lying in the middle of Europe and bearing the benefits of integration, it has made a decision for a product that is driven by Spain, Italy, Germany and England, knowing that it is a European solution and thus a future solution, and knowing that, when it comes to the industrial policy signal, i.e. the counter-business, it will have very important effects on the one hand for the aviation sector, on the other hand for the automotive cluster and for some other areas. What I say quite openly – you're right: Of course, we buy interceptors and no counter-businesses. But these counter-businesses, of course, also need a door opener, and this investment decision is such an important door opener for us. I would like to point out that in the Ministry of Economic Affairs, which is responsible for the whole field of counter-business, there is a study by the Institute of Industrial Sciences, which has calculated synergies that have been achieved by all counter-contracts carried out since 1978 by military orders. These are very interesting figures: secure jobs: 46 800, Social security contributions: € 523 million, tax revenue: € 1 146 million, positive effect on the trade balance: €2.3 billion, total value added: €4.08 billion. All of this through counter-contracts for military orders since 1978. I would like to refer to the procurement of the Black Hawk, which is about counter-businesses of 200 percent. Specific examples before the "Black Hawk" was delivered: Pankl Racing in Bruck an der Mur: Heckrotoran drive for the helicopter by € 11.5 million, Tirol Plansee Group: High-temperature materials, FACC – Fischer Advanced Composite Components in Upper Austria: Airplane parts. A total of €42 million were completed with local companies before the first Black Hawk compensation transactions were delivered. This is just to show that we can achieve an important effect for the economy here – provided that we buy interception hunters and not counter-businesses. But I also say quite openly to you: I'm really happy, I'm really happy about every counter-business we can achieve. We will do everything we can in the negotiations to optimize the price in order to optimize supply conditions in order to give rise to the best possible effect by the counter-businesses for small and medium-sized enterprises, for the industry of this country, so that there will again be an employment effect for Austria during this time. What is the goal of Natura 2000? – Protect the diversity of plant species and wildlife species and habitats close to nature. As an example, I would like to quote my state of Salzburg. Since 1995, 24 landscapes or strips of land have been incorporated into the protected area. Two are still outstanding, and as far as I am aware, this will be done over the summer. This means that the EU's stated objective has been achieved: 15 percent of the total land area lies in the protected area. I assume that in the final development, there are even three or four further areas to be added. This is a Salzburg way, which outlines above all one thing: The conversation with the property owners concerned is being sought, thus contributing to the development of the Natura 2000 areas. The property owners are those who have to enter into such obligations. But there are not only obligations, but also commandments. Management bids must be standardised to ensure that Natura 2000 sites are protected as such. In principle, we cannot talk about a lack of implementation, at least as far as my own country is concerned. I admit, however, that there are differences of vote between the federal states. Salzburg at least has done his homework positively. Ladies and gentlemen! It is also crucial in terms of management and management plans that environmental requirements are met, that natural habitats are protected and that their conservation is maintained. I assume, and this has also been said, that the Alpine list concerning the Natura 2000 areas will also be finalized accordingly. In the end, however, the protected habitats are also used touristically – I may mention this as the mayor of a tourism community – such as the alps in my municipality Maria Alm. It is about the core of the matter, namely, on the one hand – and that is the decisive thing – that one receives biodiversity, and on the other hand, that one may experience this intact environment and this intact nature, and not – to stick to the words of my colleague Lichtenberger – for the sickening of our environment, which is simply not at all. The Rotterdam Convention on Dangerous Chemicals allows countries for the first time to refuse or control the import of hazardous chemicals. This is particularly true of developing countries, which have so far had to deal with the problem of the export of dangerous chemicals from the so-called developed countries to the developing countries. This currently concerns 26 plant protection products and pesticides as well as five industrial chemicals, and I think this is a very important step. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] My colleague will then discuss the protocol on persistent organic pollutants. It regulates the cross-border movement of genetically modified organisms. For the first time, there is a mandatory notification if there is an export of genetically modified organisms intended for release and an information procedure has been introduced. I personally regret that there is no liability and that labelling is not included, but I hope that such a regulation will be adopted in the framework of the Conference of States Parties and that Austria will continue to do so. Overall, this is a very great progress in environmental policy, and we will, of course, give this ratification our consent.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e20287	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! These ratifications are to be welcomed, not a question. In the present cases of ratifications, however, there are basically no effects on Austria due to our existing legal provisions. Nevertheless, it is important to proceed internationally and to reach agreements at international level. The Rotterdam Convention concerns trade in chemicals and, as I believe, serves, in particular, to protect the Third World and the emerging countries. This concerns the prevention of the sale of substances produced in countries where these substances are prohibited and should therefore not be sold. As I said, this has no effect on Austria because we do not produce such substances. It is also about the protocol to the POPs Convention, that is, far-reaching cross-border air pollution. These are biologically non-degradable materials, i.e. persistent organic substances. The content of this Convention deals with the prohibition for States Parties to produce or use certain substances, such as DDT currently. As I said, this is not Austria. Rules for disposal, namely environmentally sound disposal, are part of this Convention, further restrictions on use and the setting of binding limits on new waste incineration plants. It is also a basis for the exchange of information and technology with the aim of developing and using alternatives to the persistent organic pollutants I mention. Similarly, a regular review is an obligation for the Contracting States, which shall take place in accordance with this Protocol. – The Protocol is therefore to be welcomed. I would point out that there is a very strict chemical law in Austria, which is ultimately also the reason why this Convention does not have a direct impact on Austria. But I would like to stress once again: The ratification is to be welcomed. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e20354	Mr President. Mr Umweltminister! High house! There are four international environmental agreements which are now being ratified. The Green Group will vote in favour of all four. All four agreements have made progress in international environmental thinking and in the negotiations we expressly welcome. That is why we also support the ratification. Two agreements play a major role in the area of air pollution, and I would like to take one of them out because this also has an impact on Austria in particular. It is the so-called POP agreement, and one of the pollutants that are preferably treated in it is the exhaust gas dioxin. We ourselves have held a long discussion in Austria, especially with regard to the emissions of waste combustion plants, and I am pleased that this agreement is now being ratified. I hope, above all, that it will be implemented, because in some areas there is still a need for action in this area in Austria. In Austria, for example, there is a need for action regarding the national data collection, especially as regards an emission-chamber of water. There is a need for action to improve the limitation of dioxin in the air, a regulation on the combustion of waste is pending, and there is no waste water emission regulation on dioxins from combustion gas. I do not think it would be right to ratify international agreements, but then not to guarantee 100% implementation. Therefore, I assume that the Minister for the Environment, essentially in his area of competence, will take these missing steps to fully implement them as soon as possible. A second agreement, which I would like to address briefly, is the Cartagena Protocol. This could be an aid to the future, particularly in the Austrian tradition, genetic engineering and its effects. The Cartagena Protocol was essentially a good compromise between the NGOs as well as the food industry and the genetic engineering industry, and it offers the developing countries, in particular, the opportunity to protect their eco-systems by setting up non-genocidal zones or by taking particular account of specific eco-systems. They also have legal control of this protocol. Overall, the Cartagena Protocol is a paper that expresses very well the conflict that is currently happening at international level, such as in the field of genetic engineering and its use in agriculture. Negotiations and disputes surrounding this Protocol have also shown very closely the lines of conflict. I would like Austria to be always on the right side in these lines of conflict and to be very clear and comprehensive in its representation of the critical aspects and the concern of genetically modified organisms in agriculture in such negotiations. However, this should not only be the case in international negotiations, but also in Austria one must open the way massively and free the way for a free genetic engineering zone in Austria. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e20429	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In May of this year, a conference on the Convention on Persistent Organic Pollutants (POP) was held in Stockholm. The purpose of this Convention is to regulate the production, import, export, disposal and use of the most toxic chemicals ever created. It introduces strict international controls for a first group of twelve of these chemicals, most of which are banned with immediate effect. The comprehensive POP Convention was negotiated within the framework of the United Nations Environment Programme and was completed in Johannesburg in December 2000 by delegates from 122 countries. Environment Commissioner Margot Wallström said about this conference: The POP Convention is an environmental breakthrough and shows that the international community is on the right track in dealing with chemicals that are highly toxic and are enriching in our bodies and the environment. It urges all countries to consider the signing and ratification of the Convention as an absolute priority. The Convention is a step towards a future-looking world. It is therefore very encouraging, ladies and gentlemen, that Austria is also joining this international agreement. Of all pollutants released into the environment annually by humans, persistent organic pollutants are among the most dangerous. They are highly toxic and have numerous harmful effects. In particular, they can lead to death in humans and animals and cause diseases and inheritance damage. Their effects include cancer, allergies, damage to the central and peripheral nervous system as well as damage to the immune system. According to the latest reports, studies have shown that two out of 12 cucumber and pepper samples above the limit value are burdened with the highly toxic, prohibited diet. This diedderin load doubles the breast cancer risk, which also causes the DDT very similar dietin particularly aggressive forms of this disease; unfortunately, we women are most affected. It is absolutely right for this agreement to be joined – in order to protect our people, our offspring and our environment!

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e20774	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! There are two major legislative projects to be adopted today: One is the Gas Economics Act, with which the full liberalisation takes place, and the other is a new law, the so-called Ecostrom Act. [[President Dr Fassleven takes over the chairmanship.]] When gas liberalisation, as stated by an EU directive, was sought in Austria with the GWG 2000, the first law in our opinion was still somewhat lacking. If we are already dealing with a liberalisation project, it is very important and necessary, from our point of view, that we also create framework conditions that ensure that there can also be competition, that there is an independent regulator, that there is effective supervision of abuse, and that there is something like protecting small consumers from the redistribution of small consumers to large consumers. In our opinion, the first GWG draft was not successful. We did not give our approval to the law at the time and presented our criticisms in a different opinion. Above all, the lack of an independent regulator was a thorn in our eyes. These shortcomings are now being corrected. However, from our point of view, a major criticism remains, namely with regard to the ecologicalisation of this area. It is still true that the Austrian tax system is completely blind. The use of coal and gas for electricity generation is still tax-free. But this is one point that we are putting in elsewhere, namely, within the framework of our proposals for a tax reform, with our model "controlling other – the eco-social tax reform of the Greens", and not the Gas Economic Act. The essential points that were important to us in connection with this law, namely the creation of effective supervision of abuse, the pursuit of a widespread unbundling, the use of a regulator and the creation of a situation in which there will finally be something like a competition in this area, have now been taken into account. As far as competition is concerned, in the past, I think there was only one single one that was accepted among all the requests for transit. This is now being improved. We did not give our approval to the GWG 2000. The Green Group will therefore vote in favour of the present law. Otherwise, the situation under the Ecostrom Act is different. This represents a very important law for us, an important project. We have always considered as an example the German energy supply law, which has been in force in Germany for years and has brought wonderful successes in the field of renewable energy. We regret that the opportunity now given to create a uniform funding framework across the country has not been used to make a very large, courageous step towards ecologizing energy supply. We have some criticisms about this law. What is particularly disturbing to me and what I particularly regret is that the so-called minimum objectives in this area, the minimum objectives for green electricity, will be limited, that it is a very planned system, that tariffs apply only ten years, that photovoltaic technology has a cover, which is a very bad signal, and that positive things, such as a true national electricity label, should enter into force only after a two-year transitional period. What I also criticise is that the eco electricity has become a bit sloppy, that one also wants to recognize mixed combustion plants, plants that fire fossil fuels such as coal, with a very low combustion of biomass, as eco-electric plants. In my opinion, this is a step backwards, and an unfair handling of these terms also prevents the effective and pan-European positioning of Austrian eco-electric products. All in all, I also honour the attempts to involve ourselves and listen to our proposals. That went better than in the past. We had the opportunity to make our points. I regret that it has only become a half-hearted step towards ecologizing the electricity industry. Our demand for a nationally uniform eco electricity law for German models, with very high tariffs, with a very offensive commitment to promoting this future technology, remains true. It is true that our demand for an ecological tax reform, which can bring about many points which are very detailed here, almost planned, with a blow of a solution, namely by creating a different relationship between the so-called renewable and fossil energy sources in Austria in terms of cost truth. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e21050	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In recent years, we have implemented a number of major liberalisation projects in Austria and have provided consumers with significant price advantages with the liberalisation of electricity and gas. However, with the decision-making of the Gas Economics Act 2000, we were aware that a novel of the law would be necessary at the latest if we came to the last step – the complete market opening for all gas customers – but we had to gain some experience in it before, how the opening of the market works. We had to find that she was missing there or there. The amendment therefore has these two reasons: On the one hand, we have known that it will need new game rules, on the other hand that the correction of defined game rules will be necessary. I believe that we are meeting precisely these objectives with this Gas Economic Act, which is indeed borne by all four political groups. We have the responsibility of the previous Electricity Control GmbH and also the Electricity Control Commission, which we now have usefully in "Energie-Control GmbH" and "Energie-Control Commission". I believe that, because they will now be responsible for both of them, i.e. gas and electricity, they will take the right step, namely that a regulator will monitor the functioning of this competition and also lay down the necessary market rules to that end. There are still many details to mention; they have been mentioned by my previous speakers in part. I think there is a law here that will give this market opening and thus the game of competition the right framework. We have a second decision today, which is a special feature: In Austria, the constitutional situation is that energy matters are in themselves country-related. This has also led to the fact that we have left the countries their competences in the field of electricity liberalisation in a wide range of areas. This means, for example, that the countries were also responsible for the regulation of the promotion of eco electricity, i.e. electricity from renewable energy sources, which has led to the fact that we currently have a very fragmented situation in Austria as regards the funding instruments or the funding levels. As time goes by, all the wishes have been created and ever stronger, so that these country regulations can be united throughout Austria. It must also be said that the objectives we set ourselves in the ElWOG – i.e. in the field of electricity liberalisation at the time – with regard to the percentages of green electricity in total electricity, with this country-by-country regulation and the country-by-country attempts to achieve these objectives may be missed and, secondly, cannot be achieved cost-effectively enough. I think it makes sense, but, of course, it is also very difficult – and this should be stressed – to bring together so many contrary interests: on the one hand, the claim to the greatest cost-effectiveness and cost-saving possible, on the other hand, the legitimate interests of countries that want to see eco-energy promoted and who also want to see investments made in this field in their countries, on the other hand, the legitimate interest of electricity customers that costs remain acceptable. I believe that the Austrians are willing to purchase more costs for this form of electricity, but only up to a certain limit. I mean, in total, there is also a law here, which first implements the desire for an Austrian-wide uniform system and which, second and foremost, succeeds in achieving cost efficiency. Since around 30 percent of the total amount of electricity is currently generated in nuclear power plants in Europe, we still have great opportunities to cover at least part of this amount of electricity by means of renewable energy. Rural areas in particular can benefit from additional jobs and value added. The new Eco electricity Act can further strengthen the production of energy from biomass. This opens up a very clear income alternative for our farmers to produce food. These figures relate not only to jobs in agriculture and forestry and industry, but also to the construction and operation of necessary facilities. This is therefore a great advantage for all professional groups and all layers of the population.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e22614	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! As has already been said, the second tax-amendment law 2002 stands for the debate. Two priorities must be highlighted in particular: Firstly, in the context of the examinations of all wage-dependent operations, an audit body is usually provided for. Why do we believe that this is particularly important? . We believe that this is important to authorities not only for reasons of discharge on businesses or administrative simplification. I personally believe that there will be another significant advantage: As a general rule, a case is examined by an institution and, therefore, differing views on the assessment of a case in respect of the assessment will certainly be of benefit. This results in new synergies, also in the legal sense. With this in mind, we believe that this is really to be welcomed. On one issue, my group in the committee – today we will not do this – has called for separate voting on the subject of tobacco tax. Now one could be populist and say that we are against any tax increase. No, no, no, After ensuring what Mr Edlinger has asked or pointed out in committee, namely that there is a commitment to health insurance recovery, we believe that there is no need for a separate vote. Of course, we also agree with this area, and there will be a common vote. However, let me say a few words about a further focus, namely on the area of the new VAT system in the case of construction services. Ladies and gentlemen! We therefore consider this regulation to be a correct and first step because it is being taken to combat multi-layered tax fraud throughout the construction sector. The procedure provided for in the paper – I have discussed this with some experts – seems to me to be pleasant, so little elaborate, as far as the bureaucratic aspect is concerned. The reason why we believe that this is so important is the following: Over the last few years, it has become increasingly evident that in the field of the construction industry – usually with general companies – illegalities are occurring that go into hundreds of millions of schilling, now Euro; up to a billion euros, you already estimate! These are amounts that the Fiscus cannot give up. We therefore believe that it is high time that this is regulated. Secondly, I welcome the fact that, for some time now, financial management has recognised this problem and has become active through the establishment of a group to combat these social and tax injustices. I hope that this will not only bring something to the state, but that it will also be preventive, because this spontaneous commitment is of course important and, we hope, in this area, which is not only fiscally important, but in the end it will greatly promote unfair competition, it will be a contribution to fair competition. Ladies and gentlemen! My group has already given great importance to the problem of the shadow economy in the past. You know that there is a comprehensive request from our group on the subject of shadow economy in a subcommittee. This proposal has still not been dealt with, and we expect that the next steps will now be taken in this direction. I would like to say something, ladies and gentlemen: For me or for us in the Socialist Group, it is incomprehensible that Brussels not only knows no recipes for combating the shadow economy, but also hinders the activities of the individual Member States. Mr State Secretary, I would ask you and the entire government to become active in this area in Brussels, because Austria alone can regulate certain areas, but the comprehensive problem of the shadow economy is certainly not to be addressed. In this sense, we agree with this whole proposal. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e22681	The next speaker is Mr Böhacker. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e22699	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! First of all, I would like to thank the Social Democrats for the fact that they will now vote in favour of this tax-amending law in 2002, a tax-amending law which is quite slim from the outside appearance, but which deals with important issues, such as administrative reform measures, reduction of tax bureaucracy, the fight against VAT fraud, equality of taxation and public-friendly tax administration. In essence, Mr. Dr. Heindl, there are four points: the aggregation of the examination of all income-dependent taxes and taxes, the transition of the turnover tax debt from the paying entrepreneur to the receiving entrepreneur, changes to the Federal Tax Code, which means that the legal basis for the provision of electronic tax returns – both wage tax and income tax and corporate tax returns – and, fourth, the adjustment of the tobacco tax to ensure Austrian health care. However, let me take a closer look at one point, namely the whole area of wage tax auditing, the examination of wage-dependent taxes and taxes. At present, there is still a so-called audit overkill, because up to four auditors check one and the same wage calculation. With this tax-amending law, this audit-overkill is finally made of the garage. I am reminded – Mr Khol, who is now on the phone, will certainly remember – that it was Federal Chancellor Gorbach, who then appointed Salzburg's head of state Dr. Josef Klaus as Minister of Finance to the government in Vienna, with the result – according to the tradition – that: The head of state or the new finance minister Dr. Klaus went to Vienna with a task manual, in which, in the first place, three times the red was underlined, or better said, three times black, the simplification of wage accounting and the aggregation of wage tax audits. In the future, these operational tests will take place in a balled form. It will no longer be necessary, therefore, to call the Financial Office on Monday on which the documents will be drawn up, to review the final meeting, to draw up a notice, and 14 days later, the local health insurance fund, the local health office, will report and say: With the wage-earners! Then he checks the same matter for the second time. – Finally, the test is over again. It follows the examination status determination and the notification. Then the entrepreneur believes it's finally over. Don't do that! A few days later, the community calls and says that the local tax inspector is coming. He checks the same matter again. But still the end of the flag rod is not reached, because then the inspector comes and says: That's not true anyway. The service relationship is not recognised because the managing partner is more than 25 percent of the company in which GesmbH is involved. So that's how it looks in practice. What will happen with this simplification and merger, with the harmonisation of the examination? – In the small and medium-sized areas in particular, there will be a huge time saving, a cost reduction will be achieved, but there will also be greater legal certainty. But not only for companies, for the economy there will be benefits, but also for the authority, because the corresponding synergies can be used here. There are shorter examination intervals, possible irregularities are found much faster, which is also beneficial to entrepreneurs because errors can be quickly identified and eliminated and there will be no repayments for many years. It is unfortunate in this context that it has not succeeded in creating a single audit authority. There will continue to be the 250 local health telephone auditors in Herbert Haupt's office, the 180 wage taxpayers in the department of Minister of Finance Karl-Heinz Grasser, but through corresponding cooperation all these synergies are used by me. Mr Sodian will address the transition in VAT on construction services even more closely. – In my view, not only is the fight against VAT and pre-tax fraud sufficiently implemented, but there will also be competitive neutrality. All in all, a tax-amendment law deserves the approval of all the parties represented in Parliament!

No VAT should be charged between companies, no more pre-taxes, only the final consumer should be charged at the end. This would have been a billion-dollar gift to the Minister of Finance. Indeed, in the field of construction, it was found that there is massive tax downturn, which is likely to be charged by sub-contractors to the general contractor, but which does not deliver the VAT to the financial office. At the same time, however, the general contractor asserts the declared turnover tax at the financial office as a pre-tax. The recovery of the subcontractor can no longer be effected, because it has since gone into bankruptcy. How did that work? – One founded a GmbH. To do this, you need €35 000. You go with a borrowed savings book to the notary and point it out. The next day the savings account will be returned. So there's no problem. You need a concession, a commercial permit. - Not a problem either. With a few euros you can get them. The owner of the property will leave after a few days, and then it will start. One goes into the economy with dumping prices, one does not pay a penny to the Fiscus, to the health insurance fund, to the BUAK. The sham company then executes the performance, sets the bill with VAT and does not deliver it. This is where billions are lost. In Vienna alone, as I have heard, there are more than 200 such companies. However, the bill pays not only the taxpayer, as it avoids huge sums of money from the financial office, but also the tokens pay those companies that no longer receive orders through such practices. In the future, the tax debt resulting from sub-services in the construction and construction sector should be transferred to the client. That means that the subcontractor no longer invoices VAT. This change of law is therefore a step forward for these practices. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e23461	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Minister! High house! I would like to make a few remarks about the law on Austria Wirtschaftsservice GmbH, of which colleague Dr. Heindl himself says it is a right and good law from the idea. With Austria Wirtschaftsservice GmbH is created as of 1. This year, a one-stop shop for business promotion. The merger, integration or the personal restriction of the individual promotion bodies which have previously been independent of each other into a private-law society will create a structure that will bring substantial benefits at different levels and for all concerned. On the one hand, significant synergies in administration will be achieved with this reorganisation. It is expected that an annual cost savings of €18 million will amount to €8 million, including the fact that the individual accounting and computer science departments will be merged and the number of managing directors from currently 6 to 2 and the number of Supervisory Board members from 27 to 12 will be reduced in the future. However, on the other hand – and above all many small and medium-sized enterprises will be pleased about this – this new facility brings substantial reliefs and improvements for the companies to be promoted. Every company that has taken care of a promotion knows what it means to be struck by the promotion jungle. Some eligible company even gave up before it could even start. All these companies will look forward to the day of this law's in-force step, because it means for them: a contact point, the pooling of competences, more transparency, better planning by defining the funding policy for at least two years and the establishment of citizens-friendly and service-oriented customer centres. Ladies and gentlemen! This template takes another essential step towards reform, which not only implements the one-stop-shop principle, but reduces double conductivity and inconsistencies, putting service and customer orientation first. Of course, we are happy to give this law our consent.

In Article I, we amend the Criminal Code, and as you have already read the media, this novel provides us with the opportunity to respond to the Constitutional Court knowledge of § 209 by means of an amendment in second reading. The Constitutional Court has seen it as inappropriate that a relationship between two men is first free of punishment because both are youthful, then becomes briefly punishable because one of the partners is over 19 years old, but then again is free of punishment when both are over 18 years old. This constellation was considered unconstitutional because it is inaccurate. However, the Constitutional Court explicitly explained in its statement that the purpose of § 209, namely to protect young people from too early and from exploitive sexual relations, is not unconstitutional. Therefore, not all convictions according to § 209 per se are wrong, but one must look at that very well in the individual case. In order to allow the legislator to meet this protection objective, the Constitutional Court has set a deadline until the end of February 2003. We are now following this suggestion of the Court of Justice and closing the gaps in sexual criminal law by increasingly integrating this purpose of protection into sexual criminal law. Dr. Jarolim: Finally a statement!]] But now on the real issue on the occasion of today's criminal law amendment law, on the fight against terrorism, to which you would certainly have had a lot of criticism if you hadn't got into § 207b. ==References====External links== Last September, to fit the legal systems of the civilized countries for the fight against terrorism. Two conventions are important to us: a directive of the European Union on the fight against terrorist associations and a UN convention to prevent the basic financing of terrorist associations. It is the essence of combating terrorism that it must be made preventive in order not to allow as devastating attacks as we had to suffer them at all. It is therefore also a field of tension, which is to be shown here, between the personality and data protection that is well established in our legal order on the one hand and the purpose of combating terrorism on the other. I believe that this has been well achieved with the main changes, § 278b and d, the forward-looking fight against terrorist associations by means of preventive control, by monitoring, by taking risks, and by reducing funding and monitoring according to the pattern of combating criminal organisations. I would also like to highlight a partial aspect which I would like to mention in my memory of Mrs Bures, namely the so-called neutrality threat. The situation, as it exists in the criminal law, has not been changed in any way. On the contrary: In the course of the fight against terrorism, he was extended from "warring actions" to "armed conflicts". However, it has been systematically omitted from the heading "at risk of neutrality", since neutrality is known to be a international institute – and not a criminal offence. Neutrality cannot endanger the individual, but only the state, for example, by not buying a interception hunter as a neutral state. In this sense, I think that the Criminal Change Act, along with the amendments tabled, is worthy of approval and fulfils its main purpose, namely that one can no longer accuse us of being more secure in Europe than in Afghanistan. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e27961	We're on our way to 34. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. As the first speaker for the debate, I give the floor to Mrs Hlavac. Voluntary speaking time limit: 5 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e27988	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The Statute of the International Criminal Court is known as 1. It has since been ratified by more than 60 states, including Austria. The SPÖ welcomed the creation of this International Criminal Court, which should be responsible for the crimes of genocide, crimes against humanity and war crimes. The Court of Justice will only be responsible for such crimes committed or committed after the in-force step of the Statute, and will only act if the State itself, for whatever reasons, is not willing or unable to accept the prosecution itself. As the Court of Justice is not an ad hoc court, but has a general jurisdiction, contrary to The Hague, it is hoped that it will receive the international recognition it needs in order to actually have the importance it deserves, and that its proceedings will not be reduced as an outflow of winner justice. It is therefore regrettable that the United States has expressed that they are not willing to accept the jurisdiction on American citizens. Since, as mentioned above, the Court of Justice should only act if the competent State itself fails to act, it would actually be in the hands of the U.S. itself to prevent the extradition of citizens and the conduct of proceedings against them. It is hoped that there will still be a change of opinion, because the Court of Justice will only have the international weight it needs if there is a clear statement of opinion. I would like to point out to this draft law that it makes sense to proceed in this way. However, I would like the opportunity to be used to think about material and legal regulations. Appropriate criminal provisions should also be established. The criminal code only includes genocide as its own crime, but not other international crimes such as torture, which, of course, can be subsumed under body injury, but it would be worthwhile to think about whether or not own legal provisions should be created for this very specific area. In this context, I would like to refer to other regulations such as the German draft international criminal code on the recording of war crimes and crimes against humanity in national criminal law or also to the regulations in force in Belgium. That is why I would like to sum up: Of course, a yes to the present bill, but further steps should follow.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e28037	As the next speaker, Mr Krüger is speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e28058	Mr President. Federal Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In the past, for war crimes in the course of war, there was several times the establishment of so-called ad hoc tribunals – such as Nuremberg, example The Hague – which all had the "beautiful error" that they were almost retroactively applied to crimes. For this reason, in the international community of states, it was decided in 1998 to decide on the Rome Statute, which provides for the establishment of an International Criminal Court, i.e. the establishment of a Court of Justice for Genocide in the course of war, crimes against humanity and war crimes. They wanted to create an International Criminal Court for future crimes, no matter where they happened. In the meantime, this Roman statute has been ratified by many states, as has Austria. Now a federal law is also being created for this. However, we are now faced with the fact that essential and important states of the Community of States, the international legal community, are turning away from the ratification of the Rome Statute. This distance means that some states are hiding out of this international criminal jurisdiction, and that is, of course, very negative. I remember that, I think, one of the last official acts of President Clinton, who was then part of the office, wanted to do so in the sense of ratification of this statute, while the Bush administration now vehemently distanced itself from it. At present, there is a very dedicated debate in the United States of America, which is being held in a way that we do not understand very much. There are, for example, Members who say, in the event that the International Criminal Court enters into a way that American soldiers are being detained, the US should even be authorized to launch a "liberation action" in The Hague. This has already been achieved. Even today it was again to be read that the USA is staying with it. The U.S. representative at the United Nations Security Council has today stated literally: It is quite clear that our men are not subject to the International Criminal Court and to this jurisdiction. Ladies and gentlemen! I have to say that this kind of behavior causes our stranger. Remember all things: In particular, the United States of America has tried to extend its justice to other jurisdictions. The United States of America's judicial hegemony goes so far that it is sufficient for them to find some point of contact to settle a dispute in a completely different part of the earth administratively or legally or judicially from America. At once, there is a responsibility because some screw was created by any car in the United States of America. That is to say, the US itself is trying to expand its judicial hegemony as far as possible and to transfer all cases to the USA as far as possible worldwide. But on the other hand, where it really is about a close in the international community, they hide, demand immunity for their soldiers. They go so far as to require the international community of states by saying: We subtract our soldiers who are on their way for peacekeeping and peacekeeping missions in Bosnia or wherever you do not recognise this privilege of immunity! This is certainly very unfortunate, and I cannot imagine that this is the final conclusion of wisdom when the United States of America turns out to be there. If one has read the APA reports today, one has also to note that this exclusion from the international criminal court has also joined Israel. This is equally regrettable, and the reasons that have prompted Israel to reject this International Criminal Court are clearly justified in the fact that in the course of acts of war, foreign territory was occupied, but that this occupation alone did not use it, but that one has built settlements there and the like. Now, Israel fears an application – although not retroactive, but international, one can speak of some kind of permanent crime – at the expense of Israeli citizens. Unfortunately, Israel also fades out, and if that goes on, the sense of this International Criminal Court will be very questionable. Ladies and gentlemen! We will, of course, agree, but I do not think it can be the end of the flag rod that individual nations simply say: We do not participate in this, while on the other hand they want to extend their jurisdiction and others to be subject to this international jurisdiction.

This Convention lays down the competence of the respective authorities to protect minors. With us, these are the courts and the district authorities, the youth offices are simple. The accession recognises the competence of the comparable authorities in the candidate countries. Any other state joining will bring an increase in legal certainty and a step towards the increased protection of minors.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-10-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00110_d7e29136	Mr President. High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The amendment of the Consumer Protection Act, which we are now proposing, is a follow-up to the debate on the Law on the Protection of Interests. The questions are as follows: On the one hand, how can you keep people from landing in a debt trap? On the other hand, how can we prevent the current regime from being misused by companies in particular? The current regulation, that is, § 12 Consumer Protection Act, allows the assignment of wage and salary claims for the protection and satisfaction of unfavourable entrepreneurship claims, while the guarantee requirement of wage and salary claims according to the legislative materials is not to be covered by this provision. The Supreme Court has also confirmed this view. In literature, however, there are other views. Consumer protectionists are constantly confronted with the misuse of this provision. We therefore consider that a ban is to be enshrined in the § 12 Consumer Protection Act, according to which presuppositions of contractual salary obligations are not permitted and effective until the debtor is in default and the underlying debt under § 13 consumer protection law has been effectively incurred. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen of the government parties! I should like to invite you to think about this problem, in particular about the impact this has on people. [[Abg.
